ParlaMint-AT_2007-12-06-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00042_d8e4771	Another supplementary question is raised by Mr Mag. Arbitrator. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2007-12-06-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00042_d8e4789	Mrs. Minister, you mentioned the assignment. What have the United Nations set out in its mandate for the mission as the main task due to the inconsistency of the refugee tragedy that is taking place in this region with which we are faced as an international community?
ParlaMint-AT_2007-12-06-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00042_d8e4805	Mrs Bundesminister, please.
ParlaMint-AT_2007-12-06-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00042_d8e4820	In resolution 1778 of 25. The Security Council decided on a so-called multi-dimensional presence on September 2007. It will be a mission at various levels of security that will take place in Chad and the north-east of the Central African Republic. The main task is to create sufficient security conditions for the return of refugees and internally displaced persons. There will be a total of three components – and I think it is important to present them: On the one hand, MINURCAT is the United Nations mission, which will support, in particular, local authorities in improving the security of the civilian population, ensuring international human rights standards and strengthening legal certainty. Secondly, A separate Chadan police force, which is to be trained and supported by MINURCAT, that is, by the United Nations mission, whose main task is to create a security situation in the camps and around the camps that enables people to return to their villages. Thirdly, An authorised EU bridging mission – this is now the mission we are discussing over and over again – is intended to support MINURCAT, the United Nations Mission in fulfilling its tasks. I believe that only from this form of mandate can we see that the link between the European Union and the UN – which was a very special concern to me – was also adequately formulated and planned, both on the part of the United Nations and on the part of the European Union.

I would therefore like to make an amendment to both the B-VG and the BHG. This amendment is comprehensive; I am seeking to distribute this amendment and will explain it in the following. I am starting with the B-VG; first of all with regard to the objective of the State in Article 13 para. 2, on the macroeconomic balance and on the sustainable budgets. There is little to leave the overall economic balance unchanged in the B-VG and to do so as if that were to be true of life. So far, it has remained dead right. It is supplemented by a provision on sustainable households. What bothers me here is that – although only in the explanations, but still – we define something that I do not think is very useful from an economic and legal point of view. This is the fixing of the balanced budget over the economic cycle. Economists argue about how long an economic cycle lasts and whether there is such a cycle at all. It would be enough if we could understand how to understand under a sustainable financial policy such a one as is striving for a stable debt ratio. We have received support in this regard in the hearing, namely by Dr. Margit Schratzenstaller, the Deputy Head of the Austrian Economic Research Institute. Second point: This B-VG design contains double budgets. They simply make no sense in a law providing medium-term planning. They are counterproductive, even if only in exceptional cases. Third point: The instrument of evaluation is a central part of public management. Those who say yes to the result-oriented budget policy must also give a clear yes to a fixed note in the B-VG and must not be content with keeping this in the explanatory remarks. Finally, we are adding a new Article 51e which has already been addressed by Werner Kogler. It is not at all clear why we are adopting this new budgetary law for the Confederation and not including the countries and communities. We are giving a century chance for a uniform accounting system for all local authorities and for uniform budgetary principles in this respect. On amendment BHG: This is essentially about two things, on the one hand, about gender budgeting, which is according to the state target in Article 13 para. 2 will enter into force in 2009, but after which BHG will only be implemented in 2013. We're a little worried that this could easily become dead right. That is why we have put forward proposals in the amendment to prevent this, while at the same time trying to strengthen the reporting system on this issue. The second point that seems to me to be essential in the BHG part is that in Article 51 para. 9 define the principle of transparency as one of the four principles of budgetary policy. If we look at what Austria's reporting looks like in terms of budget figures today, I have to say: We are here at the lowest international level! Budgetary performance data are something you can achieve as a bitter. But we don't want to be bitters when it comes to information about public – yes, public! – revenue and expenditure goes. [[Congratulation with the greens.]] For a democratic state like Austria, it is high time and right of taxpayers to know exactly the revenue and expenditure of the state, at least significantly more precisely than now. [[Abg. Oils: That's it!]] Transparency, not mastery knowledge must prevail! If we take this principle seriously, we must live up to it. But then I do not understand at all why the two government parties are not prepared to lay down minimum standards for the information of data to this House legally in the BHG. We could take an example from other countries, such as the Federal Republic of Germany, where there is a monthly report – in writing, 120 pages – on the state of federal finances as well as state and local finances. What still does not convince me is the two-stage budgetary process for medium-term budget planning. I have received support here in the corresponding hearings from Professor Lehner as well as Margit Schratzenstaller. I am not convinced, therefore, that it may be easy to have a strategic discussion on the measures and objectives of the budget policy in spring if, at the same time, the ceilings for the headings and the ceiling for the breakdowns – these are the old budget chapters – are to be set. In addition, I fear that we are too little time-consuming. Because if we start with a wrong forecast, there is already a considerable risk that medium-term financial planning could be relatively easily out of the row and the budget correction needs could occur very soon. Sweden has had this, but it has gone from this process again. Allow me to conclude. I have already pointed out that there is a need for cultural change, but that will not be enough if we want to have a financial management in Austria, as it has long been implemented in other countries. It will be a great challenge to implement in the second stage what we have done. To do this, we need expertise, a lot of expertise! I expressly welcome the advisory board. I also very much welcome the involvement of the Court of Auditors – and join my previous speakers in this respect – but this will be a very, very difficult task. A third step that we need for modern financial management in Austria is a state and financial constitutional reform. This is where the circle closes to 1st and 2nd. Agenda item. – Thank you very much.

[[Congratulation of the Abg. Dr. stupid.]] [[Abg. Dr. Graf: What's substantial, please?]] – I'll be right there, Mr Graf. I have openly admitted in the Committee that I myself was very much driven by the fiction – today I say – in the time when I was a member of the Parliamentary Examining Committee: The very best thing is if everything's in a house. However, that Court of Auditors report, which comes to the same conclusion – either all the National Bank or all FMA – has already given me thought to the description of the problem, the interface and the double track. Finally convinced that this is not the right solution, the capital market crisis of the summer has me. The most unpleasant situation was in Europe in the UK. There is an all-financing supervision – FSA – that was not ready to recognise that, if it is critical for individual institutions, action must be taken immediately. At the same time, there is a completely disconnected bank, which had to be put to the effort to give the Bank's Northern Rocks the help needed because otherwise the damage would be too great for the entire economy. This has worked very well in the rest of the world, especially in the area of the European Central Bank, including the Austrian National Bank. This cooperation between the institutions concerned is particularly necessary in the event of a crisis. It is also intended to provide the necessary strength in the supervision. I come to the question of further conviction: How can I make sure that not the examiner of the central bank – about which the colleagues have previously said correctly that they are not the ones with the most experience – writes his summary, gives it to the FMA, and if you ask afterwards: Friends, how was that a year later? Don't you want to do a follow-up test? Wouldn't we have to expand?, said: We didn't have an audit order! We're not responsible for that! We're just checking on order! The next one said: The FMA is guilty, where were their inspectors? – Then the troops marched there together, were there together, but at the management meeting only the other one was there again. This dual track can be eliminated by giving everything into an authority or by determining very clearly the responsibilities. That's exactly what we did. The entire current bank surveillance, consisting of all audit proceedings, annual audit, follow-up examination, money laundering audit, system audit concerning the risk, the entire audit equipment is located exclusively and clearly at the Oesterreichische Nationalbank. Point two: The National Bank may examine independently. Point three: the entire remaining part of the ongoing banking surveillance, consisting of the ongoing screening and analysis of all individual institutions – this is nothing but actually parallel to the data stock, any process, any report that exists, any quarterly balance sheet, each individual monthly report, an overall screening with a ongoing assessment of whether the risk to the institution exists that it cannot pay for a difficult situation where there is an error. Then there is a legal obligation of the Austrian National Bank to make this announcement to the FMA. The whole thing is in a documented interface, where one is sure: Everyone has to act. No one can say: I wasn't responsible for that. But it is clearly separate between the one who examines and the one who at the end decides what to do. I also think it is better for legal reasons if there is no inquisitorical principle that one determines one, one plays the prosecutor and one is also the judge. It certainly makes the judgments and findings of the FMA more decisive. For when I refer to the findings of a third party and say: I have now examined it, and I believe that there is a mistake here, and this will work to a greater extent. [[Abg. ParlaMint-AT_2007-12-06-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00042_d8e16349	Thank you very much, Madam President. My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen of the House! The fate of little Luca has affected Mr. Klubobmann Westenthaler, the BZÖ, but, as I think, everyone has affected us and triggered many emotions. The immeasurable suffering that has been added to the young child will and will be taken as an occasion for an honest effort to seek weaknesses in the protection of children from violence and to tackle concrete further improvements. I am delighted and grateful for the fact that this week many of the honourable Members in the House – and I am today as well as some of you – are wearing the "White Ribbon", which is also a visible sign that men face violence from men and that they are in a series of cases, in particular violence against women and children. Of course, it is important to clarify why, despite a close network of youth welfare providers, kindergartens, schools, health care, private institutions, sports clubs and many social institutions working with children and young people, it is always possible that violence against children – heavy and most severe violence to children – remains unanswered. It is important to ensure that this violence is quickly recognized and terminated where it takes place and is also brought to justice. Of course, too fast findings and responsibilities have to be warned here too, because there is a risk of trying to resolve a real concern in the wrong direction. It is important, however, that all authorities and bodies should work to the best of their knowledge and conscience for the benefit of children. In this overall context and in this spirit and in this attitude, in the representation of the Federal Minister for Justice, I answer the questions asked in detail as follows: I first answer questions 1 to 4, 8, 23 to 27, 29, 30, 36, 37 and 39 in a context, because there is a close relationship between these questions. Of course, the entire federal government – I think and am convinced that the House too – is the protection of children from violent over- There was a very large, priority concern. Such protection requires cooperation, a cooperative approach where youth welfare, schools, kindergartens, prosecutors and courts, as well as civil society, work together to achieve this objective. We have a task whose doing is not to thank God at zero, but there are already very good and targeted ways of pursuing this cooperative approach. It should be noted that the possibility of removing children from an environment at risk of their well-being already exists, of course, under applicable law. The police also have the appropriate powers to protect against danger under the Security Police Act, for example in the form of the ban, which is also implemented very effectively. The staff members of the youth welfare system, ladies and gentlemen, may, in case of a risk in default, on a provisional basis, put the necessary care and education measures in place under applicable law, even without the involvement of the Court of Justice, on its own responsibility. This form is also used in youth welfare in some 20,000 cases in the year. They may also apply for interim orders to protect against violence in the family as a representative of the child in court or cause their enforcement by the safety authorities. Finally, the courts have the possibility to transfer the care temporarily – before final decision-making on this matter – to another person, for example – and in general cases, to youth welfare providers. Nevertheless, it must be acknowledged that cases such as those of little Luca suggest that further improvement of this instrument must be made in the prevention of violence and also in the persecution. The Federal Minister for Justice also pointed out yesterday in the House's opinion on a urgent request submitted by the FPÖ that those instruments which are at the command of law enforcement authorities can only be used if they are actually aware of a case of violence, and that is far too small a case. In principle, it is wrong to deal primarily with those cases – with the lower number of cases – of which the criminal justice is experienced. It would be overlooked that the vast majority of violent relations today do not come to the knowledge of police or law enforcement agencies and criminal justice. So, in addition to the strict persecution of the offences that come to light, the main concern is to strengthen the area of prevention and to brighten up the great darkness of those cases that do not know. The Federal Minister for Justice has therefore advocated a revision and harmonisation of existing legal obligations. It is also important to make it clear that a modern criminal justice, reflecting on the dignity and rights of victims, cannot be seen primarily as a danger, but at least as an opportunity for the victims, in particular as to the rights of victims to security and to a fair response to the injustice they have suffered. This idea has often been formulated in such a way that, in the interests of the victims, it should be removed from a law enforcement, an advocating obligation, because this may, in some cases, prevent or reduce the willingness of the victims to access the youth welfare facilities or to other helping and subcontracting institutions. to create support facilities. But the Federal Minister for Justice makes it clear that this must not be understood as a danger, but rather as an opportunity to strengthen the rights of victims at an early stage and before the risk of repetition. – This is therefore to be examined very carefully, and the Federal Minister for Justice is a major concern of the corresponding revision and harmonisation. In addition, yesterday the Commission put forward a very concrete plan to improve existing shortcomings: It advocates a more stringent advertising obligation for all professions dealing with children in the suspicion of violence against children. In the Criminal Code, it aims to allow the child to delay the main proceedings, provided that the child can be protected from further violence. The purpose is to create scope for measures to ensure the immediate and reliable termination of violence and, in the case of the defendant's expertise, to ensure a rapid social and therapeutic action. In the first phase, the protection of the child from further violence is certainly the primary objective. The criminal proceedings may wait, apart from the necessary safeguards of evidence, to the extent necessary to protect the victim from continuation of violence and from force majeure. On a legal basis, the prosecutor under judicial control should also have the opportunity to ensure the security of evidence and all measures to control the suspect and safeguard the victim, which are necessary for a reliable termination of the violent relationship. These include instructions to the risk to stay away from the victim’s place of residence, inspection visits – important – at the child’s place of residence, a limitation of the opportunity relationship – contacts with children – resulting from the professional exercise or other regular activities of the victim. And it should also be possible for the prosecutor to give social therapeutic instructions in the case of the law of the accused. The creation of a record of the longer-term violence relationship – which is a innovation – is already at work, in the field of qualified criminal threats, certain forms of violence will be covered by defenseless persons, in particular children. The criminal provision of the quaeling or neglecting of children and defenseless persons – the § 92 StGB addressed by you, Mr. Klubobmann – is to be extended, in particular by including the abuse as a form of action and by extending the circle of committed persons. It is also necessary to review and, where appropriate, adapt the existing penalties for qualified forms of action. In all major prosecutors and courts, special powers are created for the area of violence against children – at least for family violence as a whole. In order to support criminal justice and family jurisdiction, the proven establishment of the Youth Justice Assistance will be expanded into a national and also available psychosocial justice aid in adult criminal law. With a novel to the Richterdienstsgesetz – which is also important – the importance of knowledge in the field of violence prevention will be underlined as part of the judicial service review, and this knowledge will be expanded. The victim is entitled to reparation. As far as possible, the victim's claims for damages must already be settled in criminal proceedings. In addition, however, the rights of the victim in civil proceedings are extended similar to those in criminal proceedings, in particular as regards the right to psychosocial assistance, the right to be kept secret of the victim's address of residence and the right to a gentle design of the victim's consent. In order to safeguard the victim with the law on the protection of violence, the violence prevention instruments of intervening orders of the family court should be further extended, in particular in order to extend the period of validity of such orders. I believe that the debate, which you mentioned, Mr Klubobmann, is as understandable as it is, should not lose sight of the crucial aspect of prevention. It is primarily about measures aimed at preventing child abuse, and this requires a broad package of measures, as the Federal Minister for Justice could present it yesterday and as I was able to do today in her representation. The specific timetable will still be to be agreed in the Federal Government because, for example, a harmonisation of advertising obligations, as suggested, in professional rights, falls not within the exclusive competence of a department but within the competence of several departments. As far as the crime of the long-term violence relationship is concerned, it is important to note that in Austria we have to create a climate – and further strengthen the existing climate – which detests any form of physical violence, from the cowardly to a serious violence. With the exercise of violence, a border is exceeded, which is a very crucial border in many respects, and that must be disregarded. The new situation is thus intended to cover cases where, as a rule, those who are in need of protection, namely women and children, are dominated violently and for longer periods of time, and thus massively affected and humiliated in their way of life. A draft law will be presented during the next year as part of an overall package to increase the protection of violence in Austria. In this context, the exact design of the mentioned § 92 criminal code is also to be carried out. The Minister for Justice acknowledges that criminal law must be an essential part of the proper protection of children, of course also of women and generally against violence. However, criminal law can and will only fulfil its task if the framework conditions for the prosecution of a decent, victim interest are created. In this area, the new criminal code has made great efforts to treat victims not only with the dignity and respect required in the process, but also to grant them rights to bring the suffering suffered into the process in a protected atmosphere. The Minister of Justice is expecting a clear brightening of the dark field, which is the subject of this opposition, because victims should be more willing to turn to law enforcement authorities and this threshold will be dropped or reduced. On questions 28 and 31: With regard to the alleged blatant imbalances in the penalties in relation to property offences and crimes against body and life, the Minister of Justice replied to the urgent question on 17 November. It was already stated in October. It has distinguished between the criminal framework of the Criminal Code and the penalties actually imposed by the courts. As far as the criminal framework is concerned, the current system is characterised by a long-term and generally significant balance between property crimes and crimes against body and life, including sexual offences. These criminal frameworks are also absolutely in line with the European legal requirements, which brings about minimum penalties. In fact, the existing criminal threats do not go beyond these European legal requirements at present. For example, national implementation of the EU Framework Decision to combat the sexual exploitation of children and child pornography should be mentioned, in which the maximum penalty provided for in the Criminal Code is usually two to three times the minimum penalty provided for in the Framework Decision. As regards the level of fines actually imposed by the courts, it should be noted that the award of penalties is an independent case law, which we are all proud of, which has to be taken into account in a number of circumstances which are largely excluded from a mechanistic-linear-superficial approach. The reasons for mitigating and mitigating difficulties must be considered and therefore have a significant impact on the specific level of penalties. In the case of property offences, it is often the numerous relevant prejudices, which sometimes appear to be not applicable to the public at times. that the property offences are much higher in relation to the crimes against body and life. In this context, I would like to add that the Ministry of Justice intends to explore in the context of a study what penalties are imposed on the courts in the case of sexual offences, including taking into account the relevant legislative changes in recent years. Conclusions on the need for a change in the system of the criminal framework, Mr Klubobmann, will only be able to be taken seriously after this evaluation of the sanctions practice of the courts. Each child needs a so-called “home first order ” for a successful growth of children’s psychological circles, i.e. a first membership. If the parents cannot even agree on a main place of residence of the child, the Austrian model for the design of care after separation of the parents is linked to the consequence that the court now has the assignment of this sole concern. a parent must perform, since it cannot correspond to the child's well-being, both parents who are obviously still involved in a mutual conflict to leave the care. Since this care also includes the right to determine the child’s residence, in such cases it would not even be possible to establish a “home first order” for the child. For reasons of safeguarding and promoting the welfare of children, the Federal Minister for Justice continues to consider the current parental-based approach to care to be appropriate. A 2005 BMJ evaluation study also confirms this assessment. I now come to questions 42 and 43: The model project “Children’s Assistance” initiated by the Federal Ministry of Justice is currently running at 28 Austrian District Courts. Within the framework of this project, children aged 6 to 18 can be assisted in escalated disputes of care or visitation law. These persons have training and relevant professional experience in a psychosocial source profession. In this way, the child should be given a voice in escalated disputes of care or right of visit, enabling him to express his will and his wishes in language. This project runs until mid-2008 and is also observed, accompanied and evaluated socially. After that, it will be possible to decide on the establishment of this Institute of Children's Assistance and, where appropriate, on the expression in the future. ParlaMint-AT_2007-12-06-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00042_d8e20904	Mr President. Mr State Secretary! Ladies and gentlemen! Just a few comments on money laundering. The present amendments to the laws on banking, stock exchanges, pension funds, insurance and securities supervision will intensify the fight against money laundering. It shall be necessary to prevent the origin of funds from being concealed and to ensure that funds can be channelled from illegal sources for terrorist purposes. This novel provides increased due diligence for high-risk customers, including greater transparency in payment transactions, and allows appropriate procedures to identify exposed persons. At the same time, however, there will also be reliefs for cases with objectively low risk, as well as exceptions for donations to charitable institutions. In addition to the fight against money laundering, the novelle also eliminates, as previously stated, disadvantages for domestic reinsurers and gender inequalities in the supply of goods and services. I think, in total, that the effective improvements that can strengthen Austria's financial centre are improvements that we are happy to support.

ParlaMint-AT_2007-12-06-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00042_d8e24580	Madam President. Minister! We are talking about a federal law that amends the Environmental Promotion Act. This is mainly about the topic of water, and there are, of course, a variety of points to be illuminated. Water scarcity is thanks to God not a topic with us in Austria, except in some regions of the Marchfeld. Austria is a water-rich country, and we have the blessing of being self-sufficient. Water quality has been a problem for many years. This is why we have a water quality casserole, and in the 1970s we really got a good grip on the water quality of the lakes – and thus also promote tourism – as well as the cultivation around the lakes, and we managed to discharge the municipal waste water. That was a big step, that was a great advantage. But there are many problems left in the area of the rivers. We know that plants and animal species have also been pushed back due to the variety of cultivations and the self-cleanability of the water is not in order. This has been demonstrated in particular by the fact that we have been able to carry out the measurements on highly agricultural land. In view of the nitrate and athrazine infections that have been demonstrated, it was clear that we have a huge threat to groundwater. And that's the core of this story: Austria has its groundwater supply mainly from the groundwater sector, which is 99 percent. That is why this Environmental Promotion Act is very good in this respect and will certainly bring us forward. Something that will certainly be important – Mr Minister, I think we agree – is that the water structure in itself represents a major problem due to the morphological and hydrological changes that exist. We also need to note that these upgradings of the rivers were not a great advantage, that morphology and the moating of the rivers did not result in self-cleaning effects that we could settle birds, fish species and so on. Due to the studies we also know that only 6 percent of all river runs in Austria have a natural structure. Many mistakes have also occurred in the area of housing. We know that yellow and red zones have also been installed, unfortunately that is God's fact. We know that because of this problem conglomerate, many things have happened that were not good – perhaps also a start to thinking about the floods. I'm sure we'll have to do a lot of things. An important issue in this area is of course also the desire for access to our Austrian water; this is an important point that we must also highlight here. We have been bound to EU law on water protection since EU accession. The fact is, however, that there is great desire on the part of the EU to get the water into its hands. Here I would like to refer to a quote from Mrs Plassnik, who said there was clarity in the water. I quote her statement: In the case of water, it remains with unanimity. ==References====External links==* Official website It is clear from the Lisbon Treaty on December that, in the future, the national states, including regions and municipalities, will be responsible for services in the public interest. This includes the water supply and management of the water resources of each country. - Quote. Mr Minister, I tell you that has already happened. If you say that the water is in safe hands, I would like to remind you that this large group of Veolia, which you may know, already has access to the water in Austria and that some areas of our water have already been accessed in Carinthia. The city plants in Klagenfurt have a large share of, I think, just over 50 percent sold to this Veolia, so that the water supply in Carinthia is already in the hands of large companies in important areas and the access to the administration and communities is no longer given. ParlaMint-AT_2007-12-06-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00042_d8e24643	Madam President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! From my point of view, buying JI/CDM certificates can only be an additive tool to get closer to our Kyoto targets and numbers. First priority must in any case and always have domestic activity for three reasons: Firstly, because, of course, when we take measures in the country to reduce climate-damaging gases, we create jobs here, and secondly, we have the added value that we urgently need. For the certificates we buy, the domestic value added is usually very, very small or not available at all. And thirdly, the reduction of CO2 and equivalents in the country helps to reduce our greenhouse gas base here. Purchased certificates are puffed at the end of the 2012 period. If we lower the CO2 base in Austria, we have something of it in the long term and sustainable. It is definitely the more sensible way. A second thought about the certificates: A study was published last week, which mainly examines the CDM instrument. It becomes very clear that in about 20 percent of the cases – this study says – it is not really substituted, but rather projects that might have been built anyway, or possibly the development effects, the ownership effects, the effects that lead to real know-how transfers to developing countries are not the same as you would have imagined. I also believe that in future we will have to look at the quality of CDM, but also of JI projects, even more than we already do. More jobs also mean falling social expenditure, because unemployment is falling, while jobs we create with these investments are also high-quality jobs. And to use a term from the forestry industry: We are investing in Austria in a sustainable way. We therefore agree to this Environmental Promotion Act, but point 3a will be rejected from the considerations I mentioned earlier.

The aim is to remove permeability barriers, reduce the effects of discharges and retrofitting, set out measures against sulphur effects and restructure morphologically modified river basins. However, restructurings should only be eligible under this federal law if they are not combined with flood protection measures because they are eligible for funding under the Water Construction Promotion Act. ParlaMint-AT_2007-12-06-023-XXIII-NRSITZ-00042_d8e29769	Madam President. Mr State Secretary! Ladies and gentlemen! On the one hand, to the Red Cross Act only mentioning the fact that we decide today also because the Red Cross corresponds to a international tradition, namely the Geneva Protocols and the Geneva Conventions. This is also in line with the second decision, the Streumunition Prohibition, because the Red Cross is also one of the institutions that have always influenced international humanitarian law. 1 204 billion US dollars are spent worldwide for military purposes in this year. This means an increase of almost 40 percent, 37 percent in the last ten years. We already recognize that the global disarmament process is more than just a stockpiling process, which has almost come to a halt. The divisions of peace, which we have still spoken about in 1989, have long since ceased to exist, but the expenditure on arms is rising again to immeasurable heights. After ten years of ban on land mines, as an Austrian, we are so far to ban the cluster ammunition, the cluster union. Almost every fourth cluster union does not explode, but is almost in the field and often only comes to the explosion years later, where the victims are often children, the civilian population and carry mutilations, convulsions and the like. That is how terrible and dangerous this ammunition is, and I am therefore very glad that we are now deciding on a comprehensive ban on production, trade and possession, but also on the destruction of stocks. I think it is right and important that we also establish a comprehensive ban in that we have not excluded the smart ammunition from the ban. The definition of “intelligent” ammunition alone is more than questionable. I can't believe this. This has also been said about the incidental union, and at the latest since the Lebanon war, we know what its negative consequences were. That is why I am very proud that we Austrians also decide on a very comprehensive, clear and clear ban. We are the second country in Belgium worldwide. We could have decided almost already in the last period – it is a long-term concern – but fortunately decide today. I am glad that Mrs Bianca Jagger, who is an international ambassador and also an ambassador to the Council of Europe, has honoured this pioneering role through her presence, visit to the President and at the conference. However – and this is also important to emphasize – it is not enough if Austria decides to ban. We are pioneers and we have to make sure that there is a worldwide ban, because only then does it make sense. Dr. Schelling: Yes!]] The law prohibits the development, production and procurement of this ammunition. Furthermore, the law does not allow Austrians abroad to deal with or use these weapons. Scrive bombs are attack weapons with 98 percent hit rate for civilians. Kill and mutilate scatter bombs. As we have only recently seen in Lebanon, a violent thunderstorm with hail stroke can cause several scatter bombs to explode at the same time. This makes it difficult for the civilian population to resume everyday life after a war. I expect Mrs Plassnik to work to ensure that all States producing, buying and using scatter bombs are in agreement with an international ban. The most urgent thing would be for the other EU countries to avoid using scatter bombs in the case of joint military operations, because Austrian soldiers are not allowed to take part from now on in any action that uses cluster ammunition. The Foreign Policy Committee has noted that the Federal Government will report to the National Council after a period of three years from the entry into force of the Act on the extermination of the scatter bombs in Austrian possession. Minister of Defense Darabos has set himself the ambitious goal of achieving the destruction already in half of the time, that is, in a year and a half, and from our point of view in Austria these weapons of suffering would be the final end.
